Fake Clawdbot VS Code Extension Installs ScreenConnect RAT

Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] VS Code (Code.exe/node) drops payload to %TEMP%\Lightshot staging directory · Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution · Email attachment opened from external sender
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Office app spawning script/LOLBin child process · PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — Fake Clawdbot VS Code Extension Installs ScreenConnect RAT
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] Weaponised ScreenConnect install path with attacker instance GUID 083e4d30c7ea44f7 · [LLM] DWrite.dll Rust sideloader dropped outside Windows directory (ClawdBot redundant payload) · Suspicious browser extension installation · Scheduled task created with suspicious image / encoded args · RMM tool installed by non-IT user — remote-access utility for hands-on-keyboard · File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] ScreenConnect client beaconing to ClawdBot attacker relay (meeting.bulletmailer.net:8041) · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs · Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process
ScreenConnect client beaconing to ClawdBot attacker relay (meeting.bulletmailer.net:8041) AICommand & Control Medium
Detects ScreenConnect client/shell binaries connecting to the attacker-controlled relay infrastructure used by the fake ClawdBot VS Code extension campaign. Catches both DNS-based and IP/port-based beacons to the weaponised RMM tenant. Rationale: Anchors on three attacker-owned domains and two IPs named in the article plus the non-standard ScreenConnect relay port 8041 — combination of weaponised ScreenConnect process names with a specific port/host gives effectively zero FPs outside the campaign. Article confirms attacker generated a pre-configured ScreenConnect client pointing at meeting.bulletmailer.net:8041.
ATT&CKT1219 T1071.001 T1572
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic Network_Resolution.DNS
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Legitimate ConnectWise ScreenConnect MSP customers whose tenant DNS resolves to the article IPs (rare — these IPs are attacker infrastructure, validate via WHOIS)
  • Internal red-team or purple-team exercises deliberately staging ScreenConnect.ClientService.exe against an external relay
  • Threat-intel sandboxing tools detonating the same sample inside the corporate range
  • Security researcher hosts intentionally connecting to the IOCs for IOC verification
Suppression filter
| where not(InitiatingProcessFolderPath has_cs @"\ScreenConnect Client (" and not(InitiatingProcessFolderPath has "083e4d30c7ea44f7") and tostring(split(InitiatingProcessFolderPath, "ScreenConnect Client (")[1]) startswith_cs "")
Response runbook
Triage: confirm the host has the ScreenConnect Client (083e4d30c7ea44f7) directory and the SHA256 e20b920c... installer on disk; pull the process tree leading to ScreenConnect.ClientService.exe back to Code.exe / VS Code extension host. Contain: isolate device via Defender Live Response, kill all ScreenConnect.* processes, uninstall the rogue ScreenConnect Client instance, and block meeting.bulletmailer.net + 178.16.54.253 + 179.43.176.32 at proxy/firewall and tenant DNS. Hunt: pivot on the user to find which VS Code extension installed it (look for ClawdBot extension in %USERPROFILE%\.vscode\extensions), check for the DWrite.dll Rust loader (SHA256 d1e0c26774...) and the Dropbox MSI URL in proxy logs across the org.
let C2Hosts = dynamic(["meeting.bulletmailer.net","clawdbot.getintwopc.site","darkgptprivate.com"]);
let C2IPs = dynamic(["178.16.54.253","179.43.176.32"]);
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteIP in (C2IPs)
   or (isnotempty(RemoteUrl) and RemoteUrl has_any (C2Hosts))
   or (RemotePort == 8041 and InitiatingProcessFileName startswith "ScreenConnect.")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteIP, RemoteUrl, RemotePort
| order by Timestamp desc
let C2Hosts = dynamic(["meeting.bulletmailer.net","clawdbot.getintwopc.site","darkgptprivate.com"]);
let C2IPs = dynamic(["178.16.54.253","179.43.176.32"]);
union isfuzzy=true
  (DnsEvents
     | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
     | where Name in~ (C2Hosts)
     | project TimeGenerated, Host=Computer, ClientIP, Indicator=Name, IOCType="dns"),
  (ImNetworkSession
     | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
     | where DstHostname in~ (C2Hosts) or DstIpAddr in (C2IPs) or (DstPortNumber == 8041 and Url has_any (C2Hosts))
     | project TimeGenerated, Host=SrcHostname, ClientIP=SrcIpAddr, Indicator=coalesce(DstHostname, DstIpAddr), IOCType="netsession"),
  (CommonSecurityLog
     | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
     | where DestinationIP in (C2IPs) or DestinationHostName in~ (C2Hosts) or (DestinationPort == 8041 and DestinationHostName has "bulletmailer")
     | project TimeGenerated, Host=SourceHostName, ClientIP=SourceIP, Indicator=coalesce(DestinationHostName, tostring(DestinationIP)), IOCType="netflow")
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: ClawdBot Campaign C2 - ScreenConnect Relay Beacon
id: 8c1b4d92-7e23-4f3a-9b1c-2d5a6f8e9c01
status: experimental
description: Detects network connections to the attacker-controlled ScreenConnect relay and config server used by the fake ClawdBot VS Code extension trojan.
references:
  - https://www.aikido.dev/blog/fake-clawdbot-vscode-extension-malware
author: Detection Engineering
date: 2026/01/27
tags:
  - attack.command_and_control
  - attack.t1219
  - attack.t1071.001
logsource:
  category: network_connection
  product: windows
detection:
  selection_host:
    DestinationHostname:
      - meeting.bulletmailer.net
      - clawdbot.getintwopc.site
      - darkgptprivate.com
  selection_ip:
    DestinationIp:
      - 178.16.54.253
      - 179.43.176.32
  selection_port:
    DestinationPort: 8041
    Image|endswith:
      - '\ScreenConnect.ClientService.exe'
      - '\ScreenConnect.WindowsBackstageShell.exe'
      - '\ScreenConnect.WindowsFileManager.exe'
  condition: selection_host or selection_ip or selection_port
level: high
(source:windows.sysmon @EventID:3 AND (@DestinationHostname:(meeting.bulletmailer.net OR clawdbot.getintwopc.site OR darkgptprivate.com) OR @DestinationIp:(178.16.54.253 OR 179.43.176.32) OR (@DestinationPort:8041 AND @Image:(*\\ScreenConnect.ClientService.exe OR *\\screenconnect.clientservice.exe OR *\\ScreenConnect.WindowsBackstageShell.exe OR *\\ScreenConnect.WindowsFileManager.exe))))
(#event_simpleName=DnsRequest DomainName=/^(meeting\.bulletmailer\.net|clawdbot\.getintwopc\.site|darkgptprivate\.com)$/i) OR (#event_simpleName=NetworkConnectIP4 (RemoteAddressIP4="178.16.54.253" OR RemoteAddressIP4="179.43.176.32" OR (RemotePort=8041 AND ContextImageFileName=/\\ScreenConnect\.(ClientService|WindowsBackstageShell|WindowsFileManager)\.exe$/i)))
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, ContextImageFileName, RemoteAddressIP4, RemotePort, DomainName], function=count())
| _count >= 1
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats summariesonly=true count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic where (All_Traffic.dest IN ("178.16.54.253","179.43.176.32") OR All_Traffic.dest_host IN ("meeting.bulletmailer.net","clawdbot.getintwopc.site","darkgptprivate.com") OR (All_Traffic.dest_port=8041 AND All_Traffic.app IN ("ScreenConnect.ClientService.exe","ScreenConnect.WindowsBackstageShell.exe","ScreenConnect.WindowsFileManager.exe"))) by All_Traffic.src All_Traffic.user All_Traffic.app All_Traffic.dest All_Traffic.dest_host All_Traffic.dest_port
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
| sort - lastTime
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic where (All_Traffic.dest IN ("178.16.54.253","179.43.176.32") OR All_Traffic.dest_host IN ("meeting.bulletmailer.net","clawdbot.getintwopc.site","darkgptprivate.com") OR (All_Traffic.dest_port=8041 AND All_Traffic.app IN ("ScreenConnect.ClientService.exe","ScreenConnect.WindowsBackstageShell.exe","ScreenConnect.WindowsFileManager.exe"))) by All_Traffic.src All_Traffic.user All_Traffic.app All_Traffic.dest All_Traffic.dest_host All_Traffic.dest_port
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
| sort - lastTime
Weaponised ScreenConnect install path with attacker instance GUID 083e4d30c7ea44f7 AIInstallation High
Detects file or process events under the unique ScreenConnect Client instance GUID (083e4d30c7ea44f7) baked into the attacker-generated installer. Every ScreenConnect tenant generates a unique GUID — observing this specific one anywhere in the estate is unambiguous campaign evidence. Rationale: The ScreenConnect install directory name embeds the tenant's unique instance ID (article: 'C:\Program Files (x86)\ScreenConnect Client (083e4d30c7ea44f7)\'). This GUID is opaque, attacker-generated, and will not appear in any legitimate corporate ScreenConnect deployment — single-string match is effectively zero-FP.
ATT&CKT1219 T1543.003 T1036.005
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Threat-intel team detonating the published Aikido sample in a corporate sandbox or analysis VM
  • Purple-team exercise replaying the campaign with the original GUID intact
  • IOC backfill from a TIP that wrote the GUID into a test file for hash validation
  • Malware analyst's workstation reviewing the dropped artifact
  • Aikido or other vendor researcher hosts that examined the original artefact
Suppression filter
| where not(DeviceName has_any ("sandbox", "malware-vm", "detonation", "analyst-vm")) and not(AccountName endswith "$")
Response runbook
Triage: pull the full file-write timeline on the host — the GUID directory creation should immediately follow a Code.exe (VS Code) child process and a network connect to clawdbot.getintwopc.site for the config.json fetch. Contain: isolate the device, terminate ScreenConnect.ClientService.exe and ScreenConnect.WindowsBackstageShell.exe, run 'msiexec /x' (or manually uninstall) the ScreenConnect Client (083e4d30c7ea44f7), then delete the install directory. Hunt: enumerate %USERPROFILE%\.vscode\extensions across the org for any extension publisher/identifier matching ClawdBot Agent or with a marketplace publish date around 2026-01-27; sweep proxy/DNS history for clawdbot.getintwopc.site over the last 90 days.
let GUID = "083e4d30c7ea44f7";
union isfuzzy=true
  (DeviceFileEvents
     | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
     | where FolderPath has GUID
     | project Timestamp, DeviceName, Source="FileEvent", ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
               InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName),
  (DeviceProcessEvents
     | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
     | where FolderPath has GUID or InitiatingProcessFolderPath has GUID
     | project Timestamp, DeviceName, Source="ProcessEvent", ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
               InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine=ProcessCommandLine, AccountName)
| order by Timestamp desc
let GUID = "083e4d30c7ea44f7";
union isfuzzy=true
  (ImFileEvent
     | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
     | where TargetFilePath has GUID
     | project TimeGenerated, Host=DvcHostname, User=ActorUsername, Source="FileEvent",
               Path=TargetFilePath, FileName=TargetFileName, SHA256=TargetFileSHA256,
               Proc=ActingProcessName, Cmd=ActingProcessCommandLine),
  (ImProcessCreate
     | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
     | where TargetProcessName has GUID or ParentProcessName has GUID or ActingProcessCommandLine has GUID
     | project TimeGenerated, Host=DvcHostname, User=ActorUsername, Source="ProcessEvent",
               Path=TargetProcessName, FileName=TargetProcessName, SHA256,
               Proc=ActingProcessName, Cmd=ActingProcessCommandLine)
| order by TimeGenerated desc

id: 2c8d5e3f-1a9b-4e7c-8d6f-3b2a1c4d5e6f
status: experimental
description: Detects creation of files under the attacker-specific ScreenConnect instance GUID directory used by the fake ClawdBot VS Code extension trojan campaign. Each ScreenConnect tenant generates a unique GUID; this one is uniquely attacker-controlled.
references:

(#event_simpleName=FileWritten TargetFileName=/083e4d30c7ea44f7/i) OR (#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 (ImageFileName=/083e4d30c7ea44f7/i OR CommandLine=/083e4d30c7ea44f7/i OR ParentImageFileName=/083e4d30c7ea44f7/i))
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, #event_simpleName, ImageFileName, TargetFileName, ContextImageFileName], function=count())

| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`

| convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)

| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| append [| tstats summariesonly=false count from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where Processes.process_path="*ScreenConnect Client (083e4d30c7ea44f7)*" by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.process_name Processes.process_path Processes.process]
| convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)

DWrite.dll Rust sideloader dropped outside Windows directory (ClawdBot redundant payload) AIInstallation Medium
Detects the malicious Rust-compiled DWrite.dll (SHA256 d1e0c2...) used as a redundant DLL-sideload payload by the ClawdBot extension to fetch a backup payload from Dropbox. Catches both the file drop and the load into the ScreenConnect host process. Rationale: DWrite.dll is a legitimate Microsoft DirectWrite library that almost exclusively lives under C:\Windows\. A copy outside that path matching the article's SHA256 (d1e0c2...) or located inside the attacker's ScreenConnect GUID dir / %TEMP%\Lightshot is a textbook DLL sideload — confirmed by Cynet, Elastic, and Ikarus VT verdicts cited in the article.
ATT&CKT1574.002 T1105 T1027.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Visual Studio / .NET runtime installers staging their own DirectWrite copy under %ProgramFiles% (path-based FP — hash filter will exclude)
  • Adobe / Office apps shipping a redistributable DirectWrite in their install dir (hash filter excludes)
  • Software-update services placing DWrite.dll in a temp staging dir during patching (excluded by combined hash + Lightshot/GUID path filter)
  • Developer building Windows apps locally with their own DWrite.dll for debugging
  • Threat-intel sandbox detonating the published sample
Suppression filter
| where not(InitiatingProcessFolderPath startswith @"C:\Program Files\Microsoft Visual Studio\" or InitiatingProcessFolderPath startswith @"C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\" or InitiatingProcessFolderPath has @"\Adobe\")
Response runbook
Triage: confirm SHA256 against d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea and review which process loaded the DLL — expect ScreenConnect.ClientService.exe or one of its shells. Pull proxy logs for the same host for the Dropbox URL containing scl/fi/tmwi4j86op04r9qo2xdgh/zoomupdate.msi. Contain: isolate the host, kill the loading process, quarantine DWrite.dll, block the Dropbox path and 178.16.54.253/179.43.176.32 at proxy/firewall. Hunt: search for any process that called InternetOpenUrlA toward Dropbox with that exact scl/fi path, sweep AsepValueUpdate / Run-key persistence pointing at ScreenConnect.ClientService.exe across the estate.
let BadHash = "d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea";
union isfuzzy=true
  (DeviceImageLoadEvents
     | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
     | where FileName =~ "DWrite.dll"
     | where not(FolderPath startswith @"C:\Windows\")
     | where SHA256 == BadHash
        or InitiatingProcessFileName startswith "ScreenConnect."
        or FolderPath has "083e4d30c7ea44f7"
        or FolderPath has @"\AppData\Local\Temp\Lightshot\"
     | project Timestamp, DeviceName, Source="ImageLoad", FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
               InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine),
  (DeviceFileEvents
     | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
     | where FileName =~ "DWrite.dll"
     | where SHA256 == BadHash
        or FolderPath has "083e4d30c7ea44f7"
        or FolderPath has @"\AppData\Local\Temp\Lightshot\"
     | project Timestamp, DeviceName, Source="FileWrite", FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
               InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath=InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine)
| order by Timestamp desc
let BadHash = "d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea";
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where TargetFileName =~ "DWrite.dll"
| where not(TargetFilePath startswith_cs @"C:\Windows\")
| where TargetFileSHA256 == BadHash
    or TargetFilePath has "083e4d30c7ea44f7"
    or TargetFilePath has @"\AppData\Local\Temp\Lightshot\"
    or ActingProcessName startswith "ScreenConnect."
| project TimeGenerated, Host=DvcHostname, User=ActorUsername, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256, Proc=ActingProcessName, Cmd=ActingProcessCommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: ClawdBot DWrite.dll Rust Sideloader Dropped Outside System32
id: 3d9e6f4a-2b0c-4f8d-9e7a-4c3b2d5e6f7a
status: experimental
description: Detects DWrite.dll dropped to or loaded from a non-Windows directory by the fake ClawdBot VS Code extension. The Rust-compiled DLL (SHA256 d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea) sideloads into ScreenConnect and fetches a backup MSI payload from Dropbox.
references:
  - https://www.aikido.dev/blog/fake-clawdbot-vscode-extension-malware
author: Detection Engineering
date: 2026/01/27
tags:
  - attack.defense_evasion
  - attack.t1574.002
  - attack.t1105
logsource:
  category: image_load
  product: windows
detection:
  selection_name:
    ImageLoaded|endswith: '\DWrite.dll'
  selection_indicator:
    - Hashes|contains: 'd1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea'
    - ImageLoaded|contains: '083e4d30c7ea44f7'
    - ImageLoaded|contains: '\AppData\Local\Temp\Lightshot\'
  filter_windows:
    ImageLoaded|startswith:
      - 'C:\Windows\System32\'
      - 'C:\Windows\SysWOW64\'
      - 'C:\Windows\WinSxS\'
  condition: selection_name and selection_indicator and not filter_windows
level: high
((source:windows.sysmon @EventID:7 AND @ImageLoaded:(*\\DWrite.dll OR *\\dwrite.dll OR *\\DWRITE.DLL) AND -@ImageLoaded:C\:\\Windows\\* AND (@Hashes:*d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea* OR @ImageLoaded:*083e4d30c7ea44f7* OR @ImageLoaded:*\\Lightshot\\*)) OR (source:windows.sysmon @EventID:11 AND @TargetFilename:(*\\DWrite.dll OR *\\dwrite.dll) AND (@Hashes:*d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea* OR @TargetFilename:*083e4d30c7ea44f7* OR @TargetFilename:*\\Lightshot\\*)))
#event_simpleName=FileWritten TargetFileName=/\\DWrite\.dll$/i
| NOT TargetFileName=/^C:\\Windows\\/i
| regex("083e4d30c7ea44f7|\\\\Lightshot\\\\|ScreenConnect Client", field=TargetFileName) OR SHA256HashData="d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea"
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, TargetFileName, SHA256HashData, ContextImageFileName], function=count())
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.

| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`

| sort - firstTime

| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
| sort - firstTime
VS Code (Code.exe/node) drops payload to %TEMP%\Lightshot staging directory AIDelivery High
Hunts for the ClawdBot extension's staging step where the VS Code extension host writes downloaded executables/DLLs into %TEMP%\Lightshot before launching them detached with windowsHide. Catches both primary (Code.exe) and fallback (Dropbox MSI) drops. Rationale: Article verbatim: `const INIT_DIR = path.join(process.env.TEMP, 'Lightshot');` and `spawn(exePath, [], { detached: true, stdio: 'ignore', windowsHide: true }).unref()`. The combination of VS Code's Code.exe/node.exe writing into %TEMP%\Lightshot — a directory unrelated to legitimate VS Code use — plus the article's two file hashes and filenames (Code.exe, DWrite.dll, zoomupdate.msi) gives a high-fidelity multi-anchor hunt.
ATT&CKT1059.007 T1105 T1564.003 T1204.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • A legitimate Lightshot screenshot application installer transiently using %TEMP%\Lightshot (mitigated — the origin process is Lightshot's own installer, not Code.exe/node.exe)
  • Developer testing VS Code extension scaffolding that incidentally writes to a Lightshot-named test directory
  • Aikido / threat-intel sandbox replaying the published extension package
  • VS Code extension marketplace QA detonations on test endpoints
  • Forensic analyst extracting the sample to %TEMP%\Lightshot for manual review
Suppression filter
| where not(InitiatingProcessParentFileName =~ "Lightshot.exe") and not(InitiatingProcessCommandLine has "skillbrains")
Response runbook
Triage: pull the VS Code extension list at %USERPROFILE%\.vscode\extensions for any 'ClawdBot Agent' / clawdbot publisher directory and inspect its package.json for the `onStartupFinished` activationEvent plus initCore() / fetchConfigAndRun strings. Pull network history for clawdbot.getintwopc.site config.json fetch. Contain: kill the VS Code extension host (node.exe child of Code.exe), uninstall the extension, delete %TEMP%\Lightshot, isolate host if Code.exe (ScreenConnect installer) or DWrite.dll hashes match. Hunt: org-wide sweep for users with the same extension installed (Marketplace publisher + folder name), and for any %TEMP%\Lightshot directory creation event in the last 60 days.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2","d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea"]);
let StagingDrops = DeviceFileEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where FolderPath has @"\AppData\Local\Temp\Lightshot\"
    | where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("Code.exe","node.exe")
         or SHA256 in (BadHashes)
         or FileName in~ ("Code.exe","DWrite.dll","zoomupdate.msi","ffmpeg.dll","libEGL.dll","v8_context_snapshot.bin")
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessParentFileName;
let DetachedExec = DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where FolderPath has @"\AppData\Local\Temp\Lightshot\"
         or InitiatingProcessFolderPath has @"\AppData\Local\Temp\Lightshot\"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine;
union StagingDrops, DetachedExec
| order by Timestamp desc
let BadHashes = dynamic(["e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2","d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea"]);
let StagingFiles = ImFileEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where TargetFilePath has @"\AppData\Local\Temp\Lightshot\"
    | where ActingProcessName has_any ("Code.exe","node.exe")
         or TargetFileSHA256 in (BadHashes)
         or TargetFileName in~ ("Code.exe","DWrite.dll","zoomupdate.msi","ffmpeg.dll","libEGL.dll","v8_context_snapshot.bin")
    | project TimeGenerated, Host=DvcHostname, User=ActorUsername, Source="FileWrite",
              Path=TargetFilePath, FileName=TargetFileName, SHA256=TargetFileSHA256,
              Proc=ActingProcessName, Cmd=ActingProcessCommandLine;
let StagingExec = ImProcessCreate
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where TargetProcessName has @"\AppData\Local\Temp\Lightshot\"
         or ActingProcessName has @"\AppData\Local\Temp\Lightshot\"

              Path=TargetProcessName, FileName=TargetProcessName, SHA256,
              Proc=ActingProcessName, Cmd=TargetProcessCommandLine;
union StagingFiles, StagingExec
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: ClawdBot VS Code Extension Drops Payload to %TEMP%\Lightshot
id: 4f0a7c5b-3d1e-4a9c-b8e6-5d4c3e6f7a8b
status: experimental
description: Detects VS Code (Code.exe/node.exe) writing executables or DLLs to the %TEMP%\Lightshot staging directory used by the fake ClawdBot extension. The extension's initCore() drops files there before spawning them detached/hidden.
references:
  - https://www.aikido.dev/blog/fake-clawdbot-vscode-extension-malware
author: Detection Engineering
date: 2026/01/27
tags:
  - attack.execution
  - attack.t1059.007
  - attack.t1105
  - attack.t1204.002
logsource:
  category: file_event
  product: windows
detection:
  selection_path:
    TargetFilename|contains: '\AppData\Local\Temp\Lightshot\'
  selection_origin:
    Image|endswith:
      - '\Code.exe'
      - '\node.exe'
  selection_hash:
    Hashes|contains:
      - 'e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2'
      - 'd1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea'
  selection_filename:
    TargetFilename|endswith:
      - '\Code.exe'
      - '\DWrite.dll'
      - '\zoomupdate.msi'
  condition: selection_path and (selection_origin or selection_hash or selection_filename)
level: high
((source:windows.sysmon @EventID:11 AND @TargetFilename:*\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\Lightshot\\* AND (@Image:(*\\Code.exe OR *\\code.exe OR *\\node.exe OR *\\Node.exe) OR @Hashes:(*e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2* OR *d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea*) OR @TargetFilename:(*\\zoomupdate.msi OR *\\DWrite.dll OR *\\dwrite.dll))) OR (source:windows.sysmon @EventID:1 AND (@Image:*\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\Lightshot\\* OR @ParentImage:*\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\Lightshot\\*)))
(#event_simpleName=FileWritten TargetFileName=/\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\Lightshot\\/i (ContextImageFileName=/\\(Code|node)\.exe$/i OR SHA256HashData="e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2" OR SHA256HashData="d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea" OR TargetFileName=/\\(zoomupdate\.msi|DWrite\.dll|Code\.exe)$/i)) OR (#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 (ImageFileName=/\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\Lightshot\\/i OR ParentImageFileName=/\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\Lightshot\\/i))
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, #event_simpleName, ContextImageFileName, ImageFileName, TargetFileName, SHA256HashData], function=count())
ENDPOINT

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/

author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02

    - attack.t1539
    - attack.t1555.003

    category: file_access
    product: windows

    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'
            - '\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\'

            - '\Login Data'

            - '\chrome.exe'

        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Electrum\wallets\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\MetaMask\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Phantom\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Atomic\Local Storage\*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe","electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```

    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url

| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

              AttachmentFileName = FileName, AttachmentSHA256 = SHA256;

Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium

ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents

| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2", "d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea") or SHA1 in~ ("e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2", "d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea") or MD5 in~ ("e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2", "d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2", "d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea") or SHA1 in~ ("e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2", "d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea") or MD5 in~ ("e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2", "d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["e20b920c7af988aa215c95bbaa365d005dd673544ab7e3577b60fecf11dcdea2", "d1e0c26774cb8beabaf64f119652719f673fb530368d5b2166178191ad5fcbea"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.

Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.

Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc

LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) Actions on Objectives High
Mimikatz, comsvcs.dll MiniDump, or any non-Windows process opening LSASS.
ATT&CKT1003.001 T1003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceEvents
DeviceEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType == "OpenProcessApiCall"
| where FileName =~ "lsass.exe"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MsSense.exe","MsMpEng.exe","csrss.exe",
                                          "svchost.exe","wininit.exe","services.exe",
                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, AccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
// SecurityEvent EventID 4663 = object access. AccessMask 0x10 / 0x40 / 0x1010
// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663
| where ObjectName endswith "\lsass.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ObjectName, ProcessName,
          AccessMask, AccessReason
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: LSASS Process Access Outside Microsoft-Signed Tooling
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d04
status: experimental
description: |
    Anything opening lsass.exe with read-memory access that isn't a
    Microsoft signed component (Defender, csrss, etc.) is suspect.
    Mimikatz / Pypykatz / nanodump signature.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1003/001/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1003.001
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: process_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection:
        TargetImage|endswith: '\lsass.exe'
    filter_known_good:
        SourceImage|endswith:
            - '\MsSense.exe'
            - '\MsMpEng.exe'
            - '\csrss.exe'
            - '\svchost.exe'
            - '\wininit.exe'
            - '\services.exe'
            - '\lsm.exe'
            - '\SearchProtocolHost.exe'
    condition: selection and not filter_known_good
falsepositives:
    - Third-party EDR / forensic tools — extend the filter list
level: high
ENDPOINT

        OR Processes.process="*MiniDump*" OR Processes.process="*comsvcs.dll*MiniDump*"
        OR Processes.process="*procdump*lsass*")
       OR (Processes.process_name="rundll32.exe" AND Processes.process="*comsvcs*MiniDump*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement Actions on Objectives High
psexec / paexec / smbexec / wmic /node — service install over SMB from remote host.
ATT&CKT1021.002 T1569.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
   or (FileName =~ "wmic.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has "/node:")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by Timestamp desc

SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688            // process-create (audit-policy required)
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\psexec.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\psexesvc.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\paexec.exe"
   or (NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe" and CommandLine has "/node:")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

CISA KEV: CVE-2026-23760 — SmarterTools SmarterMail Authentication Bypass Using an Alternate Path or Channel Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2026-01-26 4 use cases 2 techniques 3 kill-chain phases detected

SmarterTools SmarterMail contains an authentication bypass using an alternate path or channel vulnerability in the password reset API. The force-reset-password endpoint permits anonymous requests and fails to verify the existing password or a reset token when resetting system administrator accounts. This could allow an unauthenticated attacker to supply a target administrator username and a new password to reset the account, resulting in full administrative compromise of the SmarterMail instance. Vendor: SmarterTools, Product: SmarterMail. Known ransomware use: Known. Federal patch due: 2026-02-16.

| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-24061")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

CISA KEV
2026-01-26 1 use case 0 techniques 2 kill-chain phases detected

Microsoft Office contains a security feature bypass vulnerability in which reliance on untrusted inputs in a security decision in Microsoft Office could allow an unauthorized attacker to bypass a security feature locally. DynoWiper deployment from shared inetpub\pub directory (Sandworm, Poland Dec 2025) AIInstallation High
Hunts for the three DynoWiper SHA-1s and their staging filenames (schtask.exe, schtask2.exe, *_update.exe) executing from or being written to a shared directory. ESET observed the wiper deployed to C:\inetpub\pub\ on the victim domain on 29 Dec 2025 and run directly from that share rather than via GPO push. Rationale: Uses the three SHA-1s ESET published (4EC3C9..., 86596A..., 69EDE7...), the unusual staging directory C:\inetpub\pub\ where Sandworm dropped the binaries, and the masquerading filenames schtask.exe/schtask2.exe (impersonating Windows schtasks.exe). All three pivots are confirmed by the ESET technical follow-up and BleepingComputer coverage. Cross-checked against: • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/dynowiper-update-technical-analysis-attribution/ • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/sandworm-hackers-linked-to-failed-wiper-attack-on-polands-energy-systems/ • https://thehackernews.com/2026/01/new-dynowiper-malware-used-in-attempted.html
ATT&CKT1485 T1036.005 T1021.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem
let DynoWiperSHA1 = dynamic(["4EC3C90846AF6B79EE1A5188EEFA3FD21F6D4CF6","86596A5C5B05A8BFBD14876DE7404702F7D0D61B","69EDE7E341FD26FA0577692B601D80CB44778D93"]);
union isfuzzy=true
( DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where SHA1 in~ (DynoWiperSHA1)
       or InitiatingProcessSHA1 in~ (DynoWiperSHA1)
       or (FolderPath has @"\inetpub\pub\" and FileName endswith ".exe" and FileName !in~ ("w3wp.exe","appcmd.exe"))
       or (FileName in~ ("schtask.exe","schtask2.exe") and FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Windows\")
       or (FileName endswith "_update.exe" and FolderPath has @"\inetpub\")
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA1,
              ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
),
( DeviceFileEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where SHA1 in~ (DynoWiperSHA1)
       or (FolderPath has @"\inetpub\pub\" and FileName endswith ".exe" and ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileRenamed","FileModified"))
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA1,
              ProcessCommandLine="(file_event)", InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
)
| order by Timestamp desc
let DynoWiperSHA1 = dynamic(["4EC3C90846AF6B79EE1A5188EEFA3FD21F6D4CF6","86596A5C5B05A8BFBD14876DE7404702F7D0D61B","69EDE7E341FD26FA0577692B601D80CB44778D93"]);

title: DynoWiper Execution - Sandworm Polish Energy Sector Attack (Dec 2025)
id: 9f3a2c1b-7d4e-4a51-8e62-1d5b3f9c4a87
description: Detects execution of the DynoWiper data-wiper used by Sandworm against a Polish energy company on 29 Dec 2025, by SHA-1 hash and the staging filenames/paths observed by ESET.
references:
    - https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/eset-research-sandworm-cyberattack-poland-power-grid-late-2025/
    - https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/dynowiper-update-technical-analysis-attribution/
author: Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/03
tags:
    - attack.t1485
    - attack.t1036.005
    - attack.g0034
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_hash:
        Hashes|contains:
            - 'SHA1=4EC3C90846AF6B79EE1A5188EEFA3FD21F6D4CF6'
            - 'SHA1=86596A5C5B05A8BFBD14876DE7404702F7D0D61B'
            - 'SHA1=69EDE7E341FD26FA0577692B601D80CB44778D93'
    selection_path:
        Image|contains: '\inetpub\pub\'
        Image|endswith: '.exe'
    selection_name:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\schtask.exe'
            - '\schtask2.exe'
    filter_legit_iis:
        Image|endswith: '\w3wp.exe'
    filter_legit_schtasks:
        Image|contains: '\Windows\System32\'
    condition: (selection_hash or selection_path or (selection_name and not filter_legit_schtasks)) and not filter_legit_iis
falsepositives:
    - Highly unlikely; legitimate IIS content directories are usually \inetpub\wwwroot, not \inetpub\pub. Verify if a custom IIS site uses that path.
level: critical

rsocx SOCKS5 reverse proxy beacon to 31.172.71.5:8008 (Sandworm Poland C2) AICommand & Control High
Hunts for the rsocx tunnelling tool Sandworm staged in the Polish energy intrusion: a binary executed from a user Downloads folder with the argument '-r 31.172.71.5:8008', and any host-to-IP egress to 31.172.71.5 (Fornex Hosting). The combination of cmdline shape + IP is unique to this campaign. Rationale: Anchors on the exact rsocx cmdline syntax 'r.exe -r 31.172.71.5:8008' and the C2 IP 31.172.71.5 (Fornex Hosting) ESET published in the technical follow-up. Both the Downloads-folder staging path and the unusual port 8008 give a near-zero FP profile. Cross-checked against: • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/dynowiper-update-technical-analysis-attribution/ • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/sandworm-hackers-linked-to-failed-wiper-attack-on-polands-energy-systems/
ATT&CKT1090.001 T1090.002 T1572
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
let SandwormProxyIP = "31.172.71.5";
let SandwormProxyPort = 8008;
union isfuzzy=true
( DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where ProcessCommandLine has SandwormProxyIP
       or ProcessCommandLine has "31.172.71.5:8008"
       or (FileName =~ "r.exe" and FolderPath has @"\Downloads\" and ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\s-r\s+\d{1,3}(\.\d{1,3}){3}:8008\b")
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine,
              SHA1, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteIP="", RemotePort=int(null)
),
( DeviceNetworkEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where RemoteIP == SandwormProxyIP
       or (RemotePort == SandwormProxyPort and InitiatingProcessFolderPath has @"\Downloads\")
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName,
              FileName=InitiatingProcessFileName, FolderPath=InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
              ProcessCommandLine=InitiatingProcessCommandLine, SHA1=InitiatingProcessSHA1,
              InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteIP, RemotePort
)
| order by Timestamp desc

DynoWiper PDB-string + vagrant build artefact in loaded modules AIInstallation Medium
Hunts for unsigned PE images on disk or loaded in memory whose embedded PDB path matches the DynoWiper build host fingerprint 'C:\Users\vagrant\Documents\Visual Studio 2013\Projects\Source\Release\Source.pdb'. Catches new compiled variants of the wiper that change filename/hash but reuse the build chain. Rationale: Pivots on the DynoWiper PDB string ESET published ('C:\Users\vagrant\Documents\Visual Studio 2013\Projects\Source\Release\Source.pdb') which is reusable across recompiled variants, plus the inetpub\pub staging directory. Variant-resilient hunting layer that complements the hash-based UC1. Cross-checked against: • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/dynowiper-update-technical-analysis-attribution/ • https://thehackernews.com/2026/01/new-dynowiper-malware-used-in-attempted.html
ATT&CKT1485 T1027.009
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
let SandwormPdbMarkers = dynamic([
    @"C:\Users\vagrant\Documents\Visual Studio 2013\Projects\Source\Release\Source.pdb",
    @"\vagrant\Documents\Visual Studio 2013\Projects\Source",
    @"\Source\Release\Source.pdb"
]);
DeviceImageLoadEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where InitiatingProcessFolderPath has @"\inetpub\pub\"
   or FolderPath has @"\inetpub\pub\"
   or InitiatingProcessFolderPath has @"\Downloads\"
| join kind=leftouter (
    DeviceFileEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where AdditionalFields has_any (SandwormPdbMarkers)
       or AdditionalFields has "vagrant"
    | project FileSHA1=SHA1, FileFolderPath=FolderPath, FileName_File=FileName, AddInfo=AdditionalFields
  ) on $left.SHA1 == $right.FileSHA1
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA1,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          AdditionalFields, FileFolderPath, AddInfo
| order by Timestamp desc
let SandwormPdbMarkers = dynamic(["vagrant\\Documents\\Visual Studio 2013\\Projects\\Source","Source\\Release\\Source.pdb"]);
WindowsEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where Provider == "Microsoft-Windows-Sysmon"
| where EventID == 7
| extend Image = tostring(EventData.Image),
         ImageLoaded = tostring(EventData.ImageLoaded),
         Hashes = tostring(EventData.Hashes),
         Signed = tostring(EventData.Signed),
         OriginalFileName = tostring(EventData.OriginalFileName),
         Description = tostring(EventData.Description)
| where (ImageLoaded has_any (SandwormPdbMarkers)
         or Description has "vagrant"
         or Image has @"\inetpub\pub\")
   and Signed != "true"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Image, ImageLoaded, Hashes, Signed, OriginalFileName, Description
title: DynoWiper Build Artefact - Vagrant PDB Path String
id: 2c8f0b91-6d34-4e7f-8b25-9a4d1e3c7f02
description: Detects PE images whose embedded PDB string references the DynoWiper Vagrant build host ('C:\Users\vagrant\Documents\Visual Studio 2013\Projects\Source\Release\Source.pdb') reported by ESET, or that load from C:\inetpub\pub\. Catches recompiled variants that change hash but share the build chain.

    - https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/dynowiper-update-technical-analysis-attribution/

    selection_pdb:
        ImageLoaded|contains:
            - '\vagrant\Documents\Visual Studio 2013\Projects\Source\Release\Source.pdb'

| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem where Filesystem.file_name="*.exe" OR Filesystem.file_name="*.dll" by Filesystem.dest Filesystem.file_path Filesystem.file_name Filesystem.file_hash | `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)` | join type=left file_path [ search index=sysmon EventCode=7 OR EventCode=11 ("vagrant\\Documents\\Visual Studio 2013\\Projects\\Source" OR "\\Source.pdb") | rename ImageLoaded as file_path | fields file_path Image CommandLine ] | where isnotnull(Image) OR like(file_path,"%inetpub\\pub\\%")

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("4ec3c90846af6b79ee1a5188eefa3fd21f6d4cf6") or SHA1 in~ ("4ec3c90846af6b79ee1a5188eefa3fd21f6d4cf6") or MD5 in~ ("4ec3c90846af6b79ee1a5188eefa3fd21f6d4cf6")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("4ec3c90846af6b79ee1a5188eefa3fd21f6d4cf6") or SHA1 in~ ("4ec3c90846af6b79ee1a5188eefa3fd21f6d4cf6") or MD5 in~ ("4ec3c90846af6b79ee1a5188eefa3fd21f6d4cf6")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["4ec3c90846af6b79ee1a5188eefa3fd21f6d4cf6"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

G_Wagon: npm Package Deploys Python Stealer Targeting 100+ Crypto Wallets

Aikido
2026-01-23 13 use cases 4 techniques 7 kill-chain phases detected

npm package ansi-universal-ui delivers GWagon infostealer targeting 100+ crypto wallets, browser credentials, and cloud keys. We analyzed all 10 versions as the attacker iterated in real-time. Category: Vulnerabilities & Threats

Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] G_Wagon dropper: node.exe spawns system tar.exe extracting from stdin (-x -f - -C)
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — G_Wagon: npm Package Deploys Python Stealer Targeting 100+ Crypto Wallets
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] G_Wagon npm postinstall spawns python with stdin pipe (fileless payload exec) · [LLM] G_Wagon Python runtime drop into npm cache with lib_core/renderer or python_runtime paths · Suspicious browser extension installation
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] G_Wagon C2 beacon: node.exe or python.exe egress to Appwrite storage buckets · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs · Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process · OAuth consent / suspicious app grant
G_Wagon npm postinstall spawns python with stdin pipe (fileless payload exec) AIInstallation High
Hunts the v1.4.0+ G_Wagon execution pattern where node.exe (running an npm postinstall hook for ansi-universal-ui) launches a locally-extracted python binary with only '-' as argument so the payload is piped via stdin and never touches disk. Filters out python binaries living in standard install roots. Rationale: Anchors on the v1.4.0+ G_Wagon exec primitive verbatim from the article: spawn(LOCAL_PYTHON_BIN, ['-'], { stdio: ['pipe', 'inherit', 'inherit'] }). node.exe parent + python child + lone '-' argument + non-system python path is a near-deterministic signature for fileless npm-dropper payloads.
ATT&CKT1059.006 T1195.002 T1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • node-gyp or prebuild binary toolchain shelling out to a bundled python for native module builds
  • Electron build pipelines using @electron/asar or python helper scripts launched with stdin pipes
  • Internal dev tooling that pipes generated python into a local python via spawn() (common in code-gen test harnesses)
  • Cypress/Playwright fixtures that orchestrate python helpers
  • Conda-managed venv whose path doesn't match the legit-path filter
Suppression filter
and FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Program Files\Python" and FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Python" and FolderPath !contains @"\Anaconda" and FolderPath !contains @"\Miniconda" and InitiatingProcessCommandLine !contains "node-gyp" and InitiatingProcessCommandLine !contains "prebuild-install"
Response runbook
1) Triage: pull InitiatingProcessCommandLine for the node.exe parent and the working directory — confirm it's an npm install / postinstall context (e.g. cwd under node_modules\ansi-universal-ui or running from `npm exec`). Pivot to package.json on disk and check for self-referential dependency. 2) Contain: kill the python.exe + node.exe tree, quarantine the entire node_modules folder, revoke any developer/CI credentials, browser-saved passwords, npm and GitHub tokens that were accessible to the user. 3) Hunt: search 30d of DeviceNetworkEvents for any beacon to *.cloud.appwrite.io from this host, query DeviceFileEvents for the lib_core\renderer / python_runtime drop paths, and enumerate every npm package installed in the last 60d for postinstall hooks that spawn tar or python.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName =~ "node.exe"
| where FileName matches regex @"(?i)^python(w|3)?\.exe$"
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)python(w|3)?(\.exe)?""?\s+-\s*$"
| where FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Program Files\Python"
   and FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Program Files (x86)\Python"
   and FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Python"
   and FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Windows"
   and FolderPath !contains @"\Anaconda"
   and FolderPath !contains @"\Miniconda"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FolderPath, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessParentFileName, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ParentProcessName endswith @"\node.exe"
| where ActingProcessName matches regex @"(?i)\\python(w|3)?\.exe$"
| where ActingProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)python(w|3)?(\.exe)?""?\s+-\s*$"
| where ActingProcessName !startswith @"C:\Program Files\Python"
   and ActingProcessName !startswith @"C:\Python"
   and ActingProcessName !startswith @"C:\Windows"
   and ActingProcessName !contains @"\Anaconda"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName,
          ActingProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine, SHA256
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: G_Wagon npm Package Fileless Python Execution via Node Stdin Pipe
id: 9c3a2b14-5d4f-4a7e-bc31-7d2f8a9e0b21
description: Detects node.exe (npm postinstall hook) spawning a downloaded python binary with only '-' as argument, reading the payload from stdin. Pattern used by the ansi-universal-ui G_Wagon stealer (v1.4.0+) to keep the Python payload memory-only.
references:
  - https://www.aikido.dev/blog/npm-malware-g-wagon-python-stealer-crypto-wallets
author: Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/21
tags:
  - attack.execution
  - attack.t1059.006
  - attack.t1195.002
  - attack.t1027
logsource:
  category: process_creation
  product: windows
detection:
  selection:
    ParentImage|endswith: '\node.exe'
    Image|endswith:
      - '\python.exe'
      - '\pythonw.exe'
      - '\python3.exe'
    CommandLine|re: '(?i)python(w|3)?(\.exe)?"?\s+-\s*$'
  filter_legit_path:
    Image|startswith:
      - 'C:\Program Files\Python'
      - 'C:\Program Files (x86)\Python'
      - 'C:\Python'
  filter_legit_envs:
    Image|contains:
      - '\Anaconda'
      - '\Miniconda'
      - '\conda\'
  condition: selection and not 1 of filter_legit_*
level: high
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:1 @ParentImage:(*\\node.exe OR *\\Node.exe OR *\\NODE.EXE) @Image:(*\\python.exe OR *\\Python.exe OR *\\PYTHON.EXE OR *\\pythonw.exe OR *\\python3.exe) @CommandLine:(*python.exe\ \- OR *python.exe\"\ \- OR *pythonw.exe\ \- OR *python3.exe\ \-) -@Image:(*\\Program\ Files\\Python* OR *\\Anaconda* OR *\\Miniconda*)
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 ParentBaseFileName=/node\.exe$/i ImageFileName=/\\(python|pythonw|python3)\.exe$/i
| regex("(?i)python(w|3)?(\\.exe)?\"?\\s+-\\s*$", field=CommandLine)
| NOT ImageFileName=/\\(Program Files|Python\d+|Anaconda|Miniconda)\\/i
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, ImageFileName, CommandLine, ParentImageFileName], function=count())
ENDPOINT

G_Wagon dropper: node.exe spawns system tar.exe extracting from stdin (-x -f - -C) AIDelivery High
Hunts the G_Wagon delivery primitive where node.exe (npm postinstall for ansi-universal-ui) drops the npm tar dependency and instead pipes an HTTPS response into the Windows built-in tar.exe via stdin to extract a Python runtime tarball. The '-f -' (read archive from stdin) + '-C <cachedir>' combo spawned by node.exe is highly unusual outside this campaign. Rationale: Verbatim from the article diff: spawn('tar', ['-x', '-f', '-', '-C', CACHE_DIR]) replacing require('tar').extract piped from an HTTPS response. node.exe → tar.exe with '-f -' (stdin source) + '-C <dir>' is essentially never seen in benign builds; it's the campaign's intentional dependency-shedding move and a strong primary IOC.
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.007 T1140 T1105
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Bespoke developer tooling that pipes tarballs over HTTP and extracts via system tar (rare, usually present in CI scripts not on workstations)
  • Some Electron / native-module installers may stream tar via stdin instead of using the npm tar package
  • Manual debugging by a developer running 'curl | tar -x -f - -C ...' under a node shell wrapper
  • Legitimate self-update flows in some Electron apps that bundle their own update tarball delivery
Suppression filter
and not InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("node-gyp", "prebuild-install", "@electron/get") and not ProcessCommandLine has_any ("node_modules\\@electron", "node_modules\\node-gyp")
Response runbook
1) Triage: capture the full ProcessCommandLine (the -C target reveals where the tarball is being extracted to — frequently a per-user cache dir). Check the npm install context: pull `npm ls` and identify which package's postinstall is in flight. 2) Contain: kill the node.exe → tar.exe tree, snapshot the -C target directory for forensics, then quarantine the entire offending node_modules subtree and force the user out of npm + GitHub sessions. 3) Hunt: look back 30d for the same node→tar pattern on this host and across the org, pivot any extracted python.exe SHA256 through threat intel, and check if the same package name (or other versions starting at v1.0.0) appear in your registry-mirror logs.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName =~ "node.exe"
| where FileName =~ "tar.exe"
| where ProcessCommandLine has "-x" and ProcessCommandLine has "-C"
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\s-f\s+-(\s|$)"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FolderPath, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessParentFileName, InitiatingProcessParentCreationTime, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ParentProcessName endswith @"\node.exe"
| where ActingProcessName endswith @"\tar.exe"
| where ActingProcessCommandLine has "-x" and ActingProcessCommandLine has "-C"
| where ActingProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\s-f\s+-(\s|$)"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName,
          ActingProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: G_Wagon npm Dropper Spawns System tar.exe Reading From Stdin

description: Detects node.exe (npm postinstall hook) spawning the Windows built-in tar.exe with stdin extraction flags ('-x -f - -C <dir>'). Used by the ansi-universal-ui G_Wagon dropper to extract a Python runtime tarball streamed from Appwrite without an npm tar dependency, reducing detection surface.

G_Wagon C2 beacon: node.exe or python.exe egress to Appwrite storage buckets AICommand & Control High
Hunts the G_Wagon command-and-control / exfiltration channel: node.exe (npm dropper) downloading the base64 Python payload from an Appwrite storage bucket, and the dropped python.exe later exfiltrating stolen browser / wallet / cloud creds back to the same Appwrite tenant. The hard-coded bucket / project / file IDs from v1.3.5+ act as a high-fidelity signature. Rationale: Anchors on the verbatim C2 URL from v1.3.5+: https://nyc.cloud.appwrite.io/v1/storage/buckets/688625a0000f8a1b71e8/files/69732d9c000042399d88/view?project=6886229e003d46469fab, plus the v1.4.0+ pivot to fra.cloud.appwrite.io. The three opaque IDs (bucket / file / project) won't collide with benign Appwrite tenants and turn this into a near-IOC-grade alert; the broader domain match catches future ID rotations.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1102.002 T1567.002 T1132.001
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic Web.Web
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Internal developer building against the legitimate Appwrite BaaS using the Appwrite Node SDK (broad-domain hit only, not the hard-coded IDs)
  • Electron app embedding an Appwrite client for backend storage
  • Penetration-test traffic from a security team intentionally calling cloud.appwrite.io
  • Mobile-emulator dev workflow where node-driven scripts query the developer's own Appwrite tenant
Suppression filter
and not (RemoteUrl has "appwrite.io" and InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("appwrite-cli", "@appwrite/sdk", "node_modules\\appwrite\\")) and not RemoteUrl matches regex @"(?i)cloud\.appwrite\.io/v1/(account|teams|databases)/"
Response runbook
1) Triage: examine RemoteUrl for the exact bucket ID 688625a0000f8a1b71e8 or project 6886229e003d46469fab — confirmed hit means it is G_Wagon. Pull the InitiatingProcessFolderPath for node/python — if it sits under node_modules\ansi-universal-ui or a per-user npm cache, treat as confirmed. 2) Contain: block both Appwrite FQDNs at the proxy (nyc.cloud.appwrite.io, fra.cloud.appwrite.io) for the affected host until cleared, isolate the endpoint, force-rotate the user's browser-saved passwords, crypto-wallet seeds, Discord tokens, npm tokens, and any cloud (AWS/Azure/GCP) credentials present on the machine. 3) Hunt: search 60d of UrlClickEvents + DeviceNetworkEvents across the org for the same domains and IDs, query npm registry-mirror / proxy logs for `ansi-universal-ui` downloads, and check developer machines for postinstall hooks invoking node + tar with stdin extraction.
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node.exe","python.exe","pythonw.exe","python3.exe")
| where RemoteUrl has "cloud.appwrite.io"
   or RemoteUrl has "688625a0000f8a1b71e8"   // G_Wagon storage bucket
   or RemoteUrl has "6886229e003d46469fab"   // G_Wagon Appwrite project
   or RemoteUrl has "69732d9c000042399d88"   // G_Wagon payload file ID
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessParentFileName, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Protocol
| order by Timestamp desc
