G_Wagon Python runtime drop into npm cache with lib_core/renderer or python_runtime paths AIInstallation Medium
Hunts the file-system footprint left by the G_Wagon dropper as system tar.exe (spawned by node.exe) extracts a portable Python interpreter into a per-user npm cache directory. The article calls out two obfuscation generations of the runtime folder name: 'python_runtime' (early) and 'lib_core/renderer' (v1.4.1+). Catching the directory creation gives an artifact even when the payload itself stays in memory. Rationale: Article explicitly names 'python_runtime' (v1.3.x) renamed to 'lib_core/renderer' (v1.4.1+) and LOCAL_SCRIPT_PATH = path.join(CACHE_DIR, 'latest_script.py') in v1.3.5–v1.3.7. Combining the directory string with the tar.exe / node.exe writer process limits FPs to legitimate npm packages that happen to ship those exact directory names, which is essentially nil outside this campaign.
ATT&CKT1105 T1036.005 T1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • A future legitimate npm package coincidentally naming a folder 'lib_core/renderer' (no current popular package does this — risk is theoretical)
  • An internal dev tool that creates a 'python_runtime' subfolder during package install — possible but rare
  • An npm package that intentionally uses tar.exe to extract a portable Python (e.g. some scientific JS-Python hybrid frameworks) — those typically use vendor names like '_python', '.python' or specific tooling names, not these exact strings
  • Forensic analyst extracting the malicious tarball on the same host for analysis — predictable, can be whitelisted by responder workstation
Suppression filter
and InitiatingProcessCommandLine !contains "node-gyp" and InitiatingProcessCommandLine !contains "prebuild-install" and FolderPath !contains @"\node_modules\@electron"
Response runbook
1) Triage: confirm the InitiatingProcessParentFileName chain leads to npm / node executing a postinstall script; pull the directory contents under the matched FolderPath and capture the python.exe SHA256 for IR. 2) Contain: isolate the host, kill any live node.exe + python.exe trees, quarantine the dropped folder + the parent node_modules\ansi-universal-ui (or whatever package owns the postinstall), and rotate every credential cached on the machine — wallets, browser passwords, Discord, npm, GitHub, AWS/Azure/GCP. 3) Hunt: take the captured python.exe SHA256 and pivot DeviceFileEvents + DeviceProcessEvents across the org for the same hash; query npm proxy logs for installations of `ansi-universal-ui` (any version v1.0.0–v1.4.2 and beyond, as the actor was still publishing during article authoring); look for sibling network beacons to the Appwrite IDs in UC3.
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileRenamed","FileModified")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("tar.exe","node.exe")
| where FolderPath matches regex @"(?i)\\(lib_core\\renderer|python_runtime)\\"
   or FileName =~ "latest_script.py"
   or (FileName matches regex @"(?i)^python(w|3)?\.exe$"
        and FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Program Files"
        and FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Python"
        and FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Windows"
        and FolderPath !contains @"\Anaconda"
        and FolderPath !contains @"\Miniconda")
| summarize FirstSeen=min(Timestamp), LastSeen=max(Timestamp),
            FileCount=count(), SampleFiles=make_set(FileName, 20),
            SampleFolder=any(FolderPath)
            by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName,
               InitiatingProcessParentFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by FirstSeen desc
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventType in ("FileCreated","FileModified")
| where ActingProcessName endswith_cs @"\tar.exe" or ActingProcessName endswith_cs @"\node.exe"
| where TargetFilePath matches regex @"(?i)\\(lib_core\\renderer|python_runtime)\\"
   or TargetFileName =~ "latest_script.py"
   or (TargetFileName matches regex @"(?i)^python(w|3)?\.exe$"
        and TargetFilePath !startswith @"C:\Program Files"
        and TargetFilePath !startswith @"C:\Python"
        and TargetFilePath !contains @"\Anaconda")
| summarize FirstSeen=min(TimeGenerated), LastSeen=max(TimeGenerated),
            FileCount=count(), SampleFiles=make_set(TargetFileName, 20),
            SampleFolder=any(TargetFilePath)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine
| order by FirstSeen desc

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies

title: Crypto-Wallet Keystore Access by Non-Wallet Process
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d02
status: experimental
description: |

    category: file_access

description: |

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)

    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same

references:

author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access

    category: process_creation
    product: windows

    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'

        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] Aikido npm phishing: DNS / web request to siemens-energy.icu or siemensergy.icu typosquats · [LLM] Aikido campaign: jsDelivr CDN fetch of weaponised flockiali/opresc/prndn/oprnm/operni npm package · [LLM] Aikido npm phishing: user clicked phishing URL hosting /DIVzTaSF credential capture · [LLM] Aikido npm phishing: inbound email containing jsDelivr link to flockiali/opresc/prndn/oprnm/operni · Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution · Email attachment opened from external sender
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Office app spawning script/LOLBin child process · Fake CAPTCHA / clipboard-injected PowerShell (ClickFix / FakeCaptcha) · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — Gone Phishin': npm Packages Serving Custom Credential Harvesting Pages
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] Aikido npm phishing: direct outbound connection to RackGenius C2 (163.123.236.118) · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process · Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs
Aikido npm phishing: direct outbound connection to RackGenius C2 (163.123.236.118) AICommand & Control Medium
Detects any endpoint making a direct outbound network connection to the Aikido-reported credential-harvesting C2 IP 163.123.236.118 (RackGenius, Muskegon MI), which fronts login.siemens-energy[.]icu / login.siemensergy[.]icu. This is article-specific because the IP was used exclusively by the flockiali/opresc/prndn/oprnm/operni npm phishing campaign in January 2026. Rationale: 163.123.236.118 is the dedicated RackGenius C2 IP Aikido tied to the campaign; corroborated via certificate transparency timeline (Oct 2025 cert on *.siemens-energy.icu pointing here). Any outbound to this IP is high-fidelity TP with negligible FP risk. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/npm-supply-chain-phishing-campaigns • https://crt.sh/?q=siemens-energy.icu
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1566.002
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Threat-intel researcher manually browsing C2 from a sandbox VM with corp telemetry
  • Sinkhole / Anubis scanning hitting the IP
  • VPN exit-node pivot through RackGenius range (very rare for corporate users)
  • Pen-test team validating IOC ingestion by replaying the IP
Suppression filter
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("sandbox.exe", "threathunter.exe")
Response runbook
1) Triage: Pull DeviceNetworkEvents and DeviceProcessEvents for the source host ±30 min around the connection. Identify the initiating process (browser? node.exe? curl?) and whether jsDelivr URL paths for flockiali/opresc/prndn/oprnm/operni were accessed. 2) Contain: Block 163.123.236.118 and *.siemens-energy.icu / *.siemensergy.icu at the egress proxy + EDR network containment on the host. Force password reset and revoke active O365/Entra sessions for the signed-in user. 3) Hunt: Pivot org-wide for any DNS to the two typosquat domains, jsDelivr fetches of the five npm package names, and UrlClickEvents to /DIVzTaSF over the prior 60 days.
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteIP == "163.123.236.118"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl, Protocol
| order by Timestamp desc
(union isfuzzy=true
    (ImNetworkSession
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
        | where DstIpAddr == "163.123.236.118"
        | project TimeGenerated, SrcIpAddr, SrcHostname, SrcUsername, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber, NetworkProtocol, Url, EventVendor, EventProduct),
    (CommonSecurityLog
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
        | where DestinationIP == "163.123.236.118"
        | project TimeGenerated, SourceIP, SourceHostName, SourceUserName, DestinationIP, DestinationPort, ApplicationProtocol, RequestURL, DeviceVendor, DeviceProduct)
)
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Aikido npm phishing: DNS / web request to siemens-energy.icu or siemensergy.icu typosquats AIDelivery High
Detects any DNS resolution or HTTP request to the Siemens Energy typosquat domains used as the credential-harvest landing (login.siemens-energy[.]icu and the abandoned login.siemensergy[.]icu). These domains were registered Oct 2025 and used exclusively by this campaign. Rationale: Both typosquat domains are dedicated to this campaign per Aikido + crt.sh certificate-transparency timeline (first SSL cert 2025-10-24, renewals 2026-01-14/16/17). No legitimate reason for any corporate host to resolve them. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/npm-supply-chain-phishing-campaigns • https://crt.sh/?q=siemens-energy.icu
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1583.001
Data sourcesNetwork_Resolution.DNS Web.Web
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Threat-intel analyst manually fetching IOCs from a corporate sandbox
  • URL-scanning service / proxy pre-fetch validating the IOC
  • Re-test by phishing-simulation vendor (rare)
  • Mail-link rewrite engine following a quarantined message
Response runbook
1) Triage: Identify user + host that resolved the domain and check whether the resolution led to an HTTP/S connection (RemoteIP 163.123.236.118 expected). Inspect the parent process — confirm it was a browser context or an npm-bundled JS payload via jsDelivr. 2) Contain: Sinkhole both domains at corporate DNS, block at egress proxy. If credential submission likely (browser navigation + POST observed), invalidate all M365 / Entra refresh tokens for that account and force MFA re-registration. 3) Hunt: Sweep org for jsDelivr fetches of flockiali/opresc/prndn/oprnm/operni packages and any prior UrlClickEvents to /DIVzTaSF over the last 90 days.

Aikido campaign: jsDelivr CDN fetch of weaponised flockiali/opresc/prndn/oprnm/operni npm package AIDelivery High
Detects HTTP fetch of the malicious npm packages via jsDelivr CDN (cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/<pkg>@<ver>/resp/template.min.js or .../reps/template.min.js). These five package names had zero benign usage and were registered solely to host the credential-harvest payload. Rationale: All five package names are explicitly named in the Aikido report and confirmed as dedicated phishing-payload carriers; jsDelivr automatically mirrors them at the listed paths. No legitimate npm consumer would request these five specific packages. Verified via npm registry pages (now removed) and Deno takedown of oprsys.deno.dev. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/npm-supply-chain-phishing-campaigns • https://www.npmjs.com/package/flockiali • https://www.jsdelivr.com/package/npm/flockiali
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1102 T1566.002
Data sourcesWeb.Web Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Curious researcher inspecting the IOC via corp-managed browser
  • Security tooling URL-scan / sandbox detonation pre-fetching the resource
  • Reverse-engineer fetching the artefact for analysis from a managed host
  • Yara / ThreatHunter scanner replaying URL list against the proxy
Response runbook
1) Triage: Identify whether the fetch came from a browser navigation (victim loaded a webpage that included the script) or from node/npm tooling (developer accidentally pulled the package). For browser context, pull the prior referrer / parent URL chain. 2) Contain: Block cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/<pkg>/* paths for the five package names at the egress proxy. If the user's browser executed the payload, force credential reset (M365/Entra) and invalidate active sessions. 3) Hunt: Sweep for any subsequent connections to 163.123.236.118, login.siemens-energy.icu, login.siemensergy.icu, or oprsys.deno.dev. Search inboxes for messages containing jsdelivr.net/npm/flockiali|opresc|prndn|oprnm|operni links.
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(60d)
| where RemoteUrl has "cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/"
| where RemoteUrl has_any ("/flockiali", "/opresc", "/prndn", "/oprnm", "/operni")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteIP, RemoteUrl
| order by Timestamp desc
(union isfuzzy=true
    (CommonSecurityLog
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(60d)
        | where RequestURL has "cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/" and RequestURL has_any ("/flockiali", "/opresc", "/prndn", "/oprnm", "/operni")
        | project TimeGenerated, SourceIP, SourceHostName, SourceUserName, DestinationHostName, RequestURL, RequestMethod, DeviceVendor, DeviceProduct),
    (ImWebSession
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(60d)
        | where Url has "cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/" and Url has_any ("/flockiali", "/opresc", "/prndn", "/oprnm", "/operni")
        | project TimeGenerated, SrcIpAddr, SrcHostname, SrcUsername, Url, HttpMethod, HttpStatusCode, EventVendor, EventProduct)
)
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Block the three host strings at egress + Safe Links policy. 3) Hunt: For the same NetworkMessageId, identify other recipients in the org. Targets spear-phishing at named industrial/energy company employees (CQFD Composites, Ingeteam, Emagine, Amixon, CMC America). Rationale: Combines the article's package names with the credential-harvest domains; spear-phishing nature means hits in EmailEvents/EmailUrlInfo are essentially guaranteed TPs. The five target companies (CQFD, Ingeteam, Emagine, Amixon, CMC America) suggest org-wide sweep for partners/customers in the same supply chain. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/npm-supply-chain-phishing-campaigns
ATT&CKT1566.002
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Internal SOC awareness email warning users about the campaign with the IOC URLs
  • Threat-intel feed mail with the IOCs as analyst attachments
  • Vendor IOC-sharing list (MISP/OpenCTI) auto-forwarded into a shared mailbox
  • Phishing-simulation vendor replaying the lure (unlikely)
Suppression filter
| where SenderFromDomain !in~ ("soc.contoso.com", "threatintel.contoso.com")
Response runbook
1) Triage: Identify every recipient on the NetworkMessageId (or fan-out to all matching SenderFromAddress / SenderIPv4). Check DeliveryAction — was anything delivered to Inbox vs. Junk vs. Quarantine? 2) Contain: ZAP (zero-hour auto-purge) the message tenant-wide via Defender for Office 365. Block sender domain + IPv4 at the EOP connector. Where the user clicked, force credential reset + MFA re-registration and revoke active tokens. 3) Hunt: Pivot org-wide for any SenderFromDomain match over 90 days, and for any UrlClickEvents to the three credential-harvest domains. Cross-reference with HR list of staff in procurement / RFQ-handling roles at industrial/energy verticals — they are the likely next targets.
let BadUrls = EmailUrlInfo
    | where Timestamp > ago(60d)
    | where Url has_any ("cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/flockiali", "cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/opresc", "cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/prndn", "cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/oprnm", "cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/operni", "siemens-energy.icu", "siemensergy.icu", "oprsys.deno.dev")
    | project Timestamp, NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain;
EmailEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(60d)
| join kind=inner BadUrls on NetworkMessageId
| project Timestamp, NetworkMessageId, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, SenderIPv4, RecipientEmailAddress, Subject, DeliveryAction, DeliveryLocation, Url, UrlDomain
| order by Timestamp desc
OfficeActivity
| where TimeGenerated > ago(60d)

        and tostring(Parameters) has_any ("siemens-energy.icu", "siemensergy.icu"))

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc

| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
Email attachment opened from external sender Delivery High
Attachment delivery + execution by recipient — most common malware initial access.
ATT&CKT1566.001 T1204.002
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Endpoint.Processes EmailAttachmentInfo DeviceProcessEvents
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MalAttachments = EmailAttachmentInfo
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | project NetworkMessageId, RecipientEmailAddress,
              AttachmentFileName = FileName, AttachmentSHA256 = SHA256;
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("OUTLOOK.EXE","winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                      "mshta.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe")
| join kind=inner MalAttachments on $left.AccountUpn == $right.RecipientEmailAddress
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, AttachmentFileName, AttachmentSHA256
// Sentinel doesn't have an attachment-hash table. Best approximation:
// OfficeActivity Send/Receive activity + correlate to host process spawn.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MailWithAttach = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation in~ ("Send","Receive")
    | where isnotempty(MessageId)
    | project EmailTime = TimeGenerated, MailboxOwnerUPN, Sender,
              Recipients, Subject, MessageId;
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName endswith "OUTLOOK.EXE"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Office app spawning script/LOLBin child process Exploitation High

DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe","outlook.exe","onenote.exe","mspub.exe","visio.exe")

SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName endswith "\winword.exe"
   or ParentProcessName endswith "\excel.exe"
   or ParentProcessName endswith "\powerpnt.exe"
   or ParentProcessName endswith "\outlook.exe"
   or ParentProcessName endswith "\onenote.exe"
   or ParentProcessName endswith "\mspub.exe"
   or ParentProcessName endswith "\visio.exe"

   or NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\pwsh.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"

Fake CAPTCHA / clipboard-injected PowerShell (ClickFix / FakeCaptcha) Exploitation High

Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("explorer.exe","RuntimeBroker.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","mshta.exe")
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(iex|invoke-expression|frombase64|downloadstring|hxxp|curl |wget )"

SecurityEvent

title: FakeCaptcha / ClickFix - Clipboard-Injected PowerShell from Explorer
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d06
status: experimental
description: |
    "FakeCaptcha" / "ClickFix" social-engineering: malicious site asks
    user to press Win+R and paste a payload. The result is
    powershell/mshta launched directly from explorer.exe with iex /
    base64 / downloadstring in the command line.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/001/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1204/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02

    - attack.t1059.001
    - attack.t1204.002

    category: process_creation
    product: windows

            - '\explorer.exe'
            - '\RuntimeBroker.exe'

        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
    selection_cmd:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - 'iex'
            - 'invoke-expression'
            - 'frombase64'
            - 'downloadstring'
            - 'hxxp'

            - 'wget '

      AND (Processes.process="*iex*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*hxxp*" OR Processes.process="*curl*" OR Processes.process="*wget*")

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("3ceb182fb32a8fb0f0fcf056d6ab8de1cf6e789053f1aadc98ba315ae9a96f0c", "fdb6c79a8d01b528698c53ebd5030f875242e6af93f6ae799dee7f66b452bf3e", "4631584783d84758ae58bc717b08ac67d99dee30985db18b9d2b08df8721348e", "211f88a55e8fe9254f75c358c42bb7e78e014b862de7ea6e8b80ed1f78d13add", "7d7f795ac1fcb5623731a50999f518877fd423a5a98219d0f495c488564a1554") or SHA1 in~ ("3ceb182fb32a8fb0f0fcf056d6ab8de1cf6e789053f1aadc98ba315ae9a96f0c", "fdb6c79a8d01b528698c53ebd5030f875242e6af93f6ae799dee7f66b452bf3e", "4631584783d84758ae58bc717b08ac67d99dee30985db18b9d2b08df8721348e", "211f88a55e8fe9254f75c358c42bb7e78e014b862de7ea6e8b80ed1f78d13add", "7d7f795ac1fcb5623731a50999f518877fd423a5a98219d0f495c488564a1554") or MD5 in~ ("3ceb182fb32a8fb0f0fcf056d6ab8de1cf6e789053f1aadc98ba315ae9a96f0c", "fdb6c79a8d01b528698c53ebd5030f875242e6af93f6ae799dee7f66b452bf3e", "4631584783d84758ae58bc717b08ac67d99dee30985db18b9d2b08df8721348e", "211f88a55e8fe9254f75c358c42bb7e78e014b862de7ea6e8b80ed1f78d13add", "7d7f795ac1fcb5623731a50999f518877fd423a5a98219d0f495c488564a1554")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("3ceb182fb32a8fb0f0fcf056d6ab8de1cf6e789053f1aadc98ba315ae9a96f0c", "fdb6c79a8d01b528698c53ebd5030f875242e6af93f6ae799dee7f66b452bf3e", "4631584783d84758ae58bc717b08ac67d99dee30985db18b9d2b08df8721348e", "211f88a55e8fe9254f75c358c42bb7e78e014b862de7ea6e8b80ed1f78d13add", "7d7f795ac1fcb5623731a50999f518877fd423a5a98219d0f495c488564a1554") or SHA1 in~ ("3ceb182fb32a8fb0f0fcf056d6ab8de1cf6e789053f1aadc98ba315ae9a96f0c", "fdb6c79a8d01b528698c53ebd5030f875242e6af93f6ae799dee7f66b452bf3e", "4631584783d84758ae58bc717b08ac67d99dee30985db18b9d2b08df8721348e", "211f88a55e8fe9254f75c358c42bb7e78e014b862de7ea6e8b80ed1f78d13add", "7d7f795ac1fcb5623731a50999f518877fd423a5a98219d0f495c488564a1554") or MD5 in~ ("3ceb182fb32a8fb0f0fcf056d6ab8de1cf6e789053f1aadc98ba315ae9a96f0c", "fdb6c79a8d01b528698c53ebd5030f875242e6af93f6ae799dee7f66b452bf3e", "4631584783d84758ae58bc717b08ac67d99dee30985db18b9d2b08df8721348e", "211f88a55e8fe9254f75c358c42bb7e78e014b862de7ea6e8b80ed1f78d13add", "7d7f795ac1fcb5623731a50999f518877fd423a5a98219d0f495c488564a1554")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["3ceb182fb32a8fb0f0fcf056d6ab8de1cf6e789053f1aadc98ba315ae9a96f0c", "fdb6c79a8d01b528698c53ebd5030f875242e6af93f6ae799dee7f66b452bf3e", "4631584783d84758ae58bc717b08ac67d99dee30985db18b9d2b08df8721348e", "211f88a55e8fe9254f75c358c42bb7e78e014b862de7ea6e8b80ed1f78d13add", "7d7f795ac1fcb5623731a50999f518877fd423a5a98219d0f495c488564a1554"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Auto-generated hunt targeting the specific binaries, paths, registry locations and command-line fragments named in this article. Hunting tier — needs analyst review and environment tuning before promotion to alerting.
ATT&CKT1204.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Registry
// Article-specific bespoke detection — Gone Phishin': npm Packages Serving Custom Credential Harvesting Pages
// Hunts the actual binaries / paths / commandline fragments named
// in the article instead of a generic technique-class template.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where (FileName in~ ("min.js"))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName,
          FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc

// File-creation events for the named binaries / paths
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified")
| where (FileName in~ ("min.js"))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FolderPath,
          FileName, ActionType, InitiatingProcessFileName,
          InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc

CISA KEV: CVE-2024-37079 — Broadcom VMware vCenter Server Out-of-bounds Write Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2026-01-23 1 use case 1 technique 2 kill-chain phases detected

Broadcom VMware vCenter Server contains an out-of-bounds write vulnerability in the implementation of the DCERPC protocol. This could allow a malicious actor with network access to vCenter Server to send specially crafted network packets, potentially leading to remote code execution. Vendor: Broadcom, Product: VMware vCenter Server. Federal patch due: 2026-02-13.

PyPI install of malicious typosquat spellcheckpy or spellcheckerpy AIDelivery High
Hunts pip / uv / pipx install events that pull either of the two PyPI typosquats impersonating pyspellchecker. Catches the delivery phase before the eu.json.gz Basque-dictionary payload is decoded and executed via the SpellChecker import. Rationale: Anchors on the two exact PyPI package names called out in the article (spellcheckpy, spellcheckerpy). Both are typosquats of pyspellchecker; legitimate hits should be limited to malware-research workstations. Cross-checked with the related HelixGuard write-up referenced in the article that describes the same actor's prior 'spellcheckers' campaign. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/malicious-pypi-packages-spellcheckpy-and-spellcheckerpy-deliver-python-rat • https://pypi.org/project/pyspellchecker/

Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Malware analyst pip-installing the IOC into a sandbox VM for reverse-engineering
  • Sandboxed CI job downloading the package for AV/EDR regression tests
  • SecOps automation that pulls fresh malicious PyPI samples to feed a YARA pipeline
  • Tabletop / red-team exercise installing the package on isolated training laptops
Suppression filter
| where InitiatingProcessFolderPath !has "\\malware-analysis\\" | where AccountName !in~ ("sec-research","malware-analyst","detection-eng")
Response runbook
Step 1 (triage): pivot on DeviceName + AccountName and confirm whether the installing user is a known security researcher; pull the resolved package version from pip/uv cache and confirm against PyPI metadata. Step 2 (contain): isolate the host via MDE live response, kill the parent Python interpreter session, block updatenet.work + 172.86.73.139 + dothebest.store at the egress proxy/firewall, and pull the package from any internal mirror or Artifactory. Step 3 (hunt): for the same host look back 30d for outbound to the C2 IOC set (UC3), child python3 - reading stdin (UC2), and any other site-packages directories that contain resources/eu.json.gz with a base64-blob under the 'spellchecker' key.

Python parent spawns detached 'python3 -' child reading payload from stdin AIInstallation High
Detects the stage-1 fileless downloader pattern in spellcheckpy: a Python interpreter is spawned by another Python process and invoked with the sole '-' argument (read script from stdin), receiving the RAT body through the parent's pipe and surviving via start_new_session=True. Nothing touches disk. Rationale: Verbatim lift from the article's subprocess.Popen(["python3","-"], stdin=subprocess.PIPE, start_new_session=True) snippet. Python-spawns-Python with a sole '-' argument is rare outside of test rigs and uniquely matches the stage-1 RAT loader. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/malicious-pypi-packages-spellcheckpy-and-spellcheckerpy-deliver-python-rat
ATT&CKT1059.006 T1105
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Developer running 'cat script.py | python -' interactively in a shell
  • CI build step piping a generated script into a fresh python interpreter (some setuptools custom commands)
  • Jupyter kernel restart edge cases where the kernel relauncher uses '-'
  • Fuzz / property-test harness piping generated scripts into python for crash detection
Suppression filter
| where not (InitiatingProcessAccountName in~ ("sec-research","malware-analyst") and InitiatingProcessFolderPath has "sandbox") | where AccountName !endswith "$"
Response runbook
Step 1 (triage): pivot on DeviceName + LogonId to find both parent and detached child; inspect the parent's InitiatingProcessFolderPath — site-packages/spellchecker* or any folder containing resources/eu.json.gz is a confirmation. If EDR captured stdin contents, dump them. Step 2 (contain): kill the detached child (start_new_session=True orphans it from the original parent — use AuthenticationId/LogonId to locate by session, not by ppid), isolate the host, block updatenet.work + 172.86.73.139 + dothebest.store. Step 3 (hunt): pivot to UC3 for that host's outbound to the C2, look back 30d for sibling python pids matching this stdin-pipe pattern, and sweep all hosts for site-packages/spellchecker* presence.

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

ATT&CKT1059.001 T1027

DeviceProcessEvents

| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(-enc|encodedcommand|frombase64string|-nop|-w\s+hidden|invoke-expression|iex\s*\(|downloadstring|net\.webclient)"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\pwsh.exe"
| where CommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(-enc|encodedcommand|frombase64string|-nop|-w\s+hidden|invoke-expression|iex\s*\(|downloadstring|net\.webclient)"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName,
          CommandLine, ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

description: |

Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
| tstats summariesonly=false count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files

                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")

        OR Processes.process="*procdump*lsass*")
       OR (Processes.process_name="rundll32.exe" AND Processes.process="*comsvcs*MiniDump*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement Actions on Objectives High
psexec / paexec / smbexec / wmic /node — service install over SMB from remote host.
ATT&CKT1021.002 T1569.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")

| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by Timestamp desc

SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688            // process-create (audit-policy required)
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\psexec.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\psexesvc.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\paexec.exe"
   or (NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe" and CommandLine has "/node:")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName

title: PsExec / SMB Lateral Movement Tooling Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d03
status: experimental
description: |
    PsExec, paexec, smbexec, csexec, remcom — admin-grade tooling that
    pivots over SMB. Pair with `wmic /node:` for the full cluster.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1021/002/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1569/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1021.002
    - attack.t1569.002
    - attack.lateral-movement
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_psexec:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\psexec.exe'
            - '\psexec64.exe'
            - '\psexesvc.exe'
            - '\paexec.exe'
            - '\csexec.exe'
            - '\remcom.exe'
            - '\smbexec.py'
    selection_wmic_remote:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\wmic.exe'
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '/node:'
    condition: selection_psexec or selection_wmic_remote
falsepositives:
    - Sysadmins running approved remote-management tooling — wrap with
      a known-admin-host watchlist
level: high
ENDPOINT

       OR (Processes.process_name="wmic.exe" AND Processes.process="*/node:*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents

| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes

    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process

          FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc

          FileName, ActionType, InitiatingProcessFileName,

CISA KEV: CVE-2026-20045 — Cisco Unified Communications Products Code Injection Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2026-01-21 1 use case 0 techniques 2 kill-chain phases detected

Cisco Unified Communications Manager (Unified CM), Cisco Unified Communications Manager Session Management Edition (Unified CM SME), Cisco Unified Communications Manager IM & Presence Service (Unified CM IM&P), Cisco Unity Connection, and Cisco Webex Calling Dedicated Instance contain a code injection vulnerability that could allow the attacker to obtain user-level access to the underlying operating system and then elevate privileges to root. Vendor: Cisco, Product: Unified Communications Manager. Federal patch due: 2026-02-11.

// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine

ServiceNow's Virtual Agent Vulnerability Shows Why AI Security Needs Traditional AppSec Foundations

2026-01-14 6 use cases 2 techniques 5 kill-chain phases detected

The critical ServiceNow Virtual Agent vulnerability highlights a vital lesson: securing agentic AI requires a return to traditional AppSec foundations. While AI can amplify risks, the root causes often stem from classic failures in authentication and authorization.

BodySnatcher (CVE-2025-12420) — Hardcoded 'servicenowexternalagent' Token Observed in HTTP Traffic AIExploitation High
Hunts HTTP requests carrying the hardcoded shared token string 'servicenowexternalagent' that AppOmni documented as the static credential used by every third-party integration to authenticate to the ServiceNow Virtual Agent API. Any inbound request bearing this token to a Now-platform host indicates exploitation of CVE-2025-12420 (BodySnatcher) since the credential is identical across all customer tenants. Rationale: Anchored on the literal credential string 'servicenowexternalagent' published verbatim in code-block 1 of the Snyk article (and corroborated by AppOmni's advisory). The token is identical across every ServiceNow tenant, so any occurrence in WAF, web-proxy, IIS, or EDR network telemetry is a high-fidelity exploitation signal for CVE-2025-12420. Cross-checked against: • https://www.appomni.com/blog/servicenow-virtual-agent-vulnerability • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-12420 • https://www.servicenow.com/community/now-platform-articles/security-advisory-cve-2025-12420/
ATT&CKT1078.001 T1190
Data sourcesWeb.Web
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Internal red-team / purple-team replay of the AppOmni PoC against staging ServiceNow tenants
  • Vendor vulnerability scanners (Qualys, Tenable, Rapid7) executing the AppOmni signature check
  • Security researchers / journalists hosting the IOC string on educational pages indexed by web-proxy logs
  • AppOmni / ServiceNow remediation-verification probes during patch deployment windows
  • SIEM ingest of historical PoC reproduction documents stored in SharePoint / Confluence (false positive when CommonSecurityLog captures the URL of a wiki page containing the string)
Suppression filter
// Suppress vendor scanners and internal red-team test ranges that we know legitimately probe this IOC.
| where SourceIP !in ("<qualys_scanner_cidr>", "<tenable_scanner_cidr>", "<internal_redteam_cidr>")
| where not (UA has_any ("Qualys", "Nessus", "Tenable", "Rapid7", "AppOmni"))
Response runbook
Step 1 (Triage): Confirm the destination host is a ServiceNow Now-platform instance (`*.service-now.com`). Pull the full HTTP request — Authorization header, JSON body, source IP — and validate the token appears verbatim ('servicenowexternalagent', no rotation suffix). Cross-check whether the request originated from a known integration partner subnet or an unexpected external IP. Step 2 (Contain): Engage ServiceNow admin to rotate the OAuth credential for the affected integration, disable the Virtual Agent API channel for unauthenticated third-party callers, and apply the November-2025 patch if not already deployed. Block the source IP at the WAF if external. Step 3 (Hunt): Query ServiceNow sys_audit / syslog for any sys_user record created in the last 30d with admin role attached, plus any data created/modified by the 'AIA-Agent Invoker' user in the same window. Pivot to UC #2 (AIA-Agent Invoker AutoChat Topic invocation) on the same source IPs.
// Hunt the hardcoded 'servicenowexternalagent' token across any HTTP context Defender sees (URL clicks, network events with URL captured).
let IOCToken = "servicenowexternalagent";
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteUrl has IOCToken or AdditionalFields has IOCToken
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| union (UrlClickEvents | where Timestamp > ago(30d) | where Url has IOCToken | project Timestamp, AccountUpn, Url, NetworkMessageId, IPAddress)
| order by Timestamp desc
// Hunt the hardcoded 'servicenowexternalagent' token across WAF, ASIM web, and IIS telemetry.
let IOCToken = "servicenowexternalagent";
let WAFHits = CommonSecurityLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where RequestURL has IOCToken
        or RequestClientApplication has IOCToken
        or RequestCookies has IOCToken
        or RequestContext has IOCToken
        or Message has IOCToken
        or DeviceCustomString1 has IOCToken
        or DeviceCustomString2 has IOCToken
        or DeviceCustomString3 has IOCToken
        or DeviceCustomString4 has IOCToken
        or DeviceCustomString5 has IOCToken
        or DeviceCustomString6 has IOCToken
    | project TimeGenerated, Source="WAF", SourceIP=SourceIP, DestinationHost=DestinationHostName, URL=RequestURL, UA=RequestClientApplication, Action=DeviceAction;
let WebHits = ImWebSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where Url has IOCToken
        or UrlOriginal has IOCToken
        or HttpRequestHeaderAuthorization has IOCToken
        or HttpRequestHeaderCookie has IOCToken
        or HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent has IOCToken
        or HttpRequestHeaderReferer has IOCToken
    | project TimeGenerated, Source="ASIM-Web", SourceIP=SrcIpAddr, DestinationHost=HttpHostName, URL=Url, UA=HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent, Action=DvcAction;
let IISHits = W3CIISLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where csUriQuery has IOCToken
        or csUriStem has IOCToken
        or csUserAgent has IOCToken
        or csReferer has IOCToken
    | project TimeGenerated, Source="IIS", SourceIP=cIP, DestinationHost=Computer, URL=strcat(csUriStem, "?", csUriQuery), UA=csUserAgent, Action=tostring(scStatus);
union WAFHits, WebHits, IISHits
| order by TimeGenerated desc

description: Detects HTTP requests containing the hardcoded shared credential 'servicenowexternalagent' that ServiceNow shipped identically across every customer tenant for third-party Virtual Agent API integrations (CVE-2025-12420 / BodySnatcher).
references:
  - https://snyk.io/blog/servicenow-virtual-agent-vulnerability/

author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/01/14

  selection_ua:
    cs(User-Agent)|contains: 'servicenowexternalagent'
  condition: 1 of selection_*
falsepositives:

  - Vendor remediation scanners probing for the issue

ServiceNow Virtual Agent Invocation of Hidden AIA-Agent Invoker AutoChat Topic (CVE-2025-12420) AIExploitation High
Hunts unauthenticated or weakly-authenticated invocations of the hidden ServiceNow AutoChat topic that wires the 'Record Management AI Agent' to the Virtual Agent API surface. AppOmni's PoC showed the BodySnatcher chain pivots through this internal topic once the static 'servicenowexternalagent' token is replayed, so any external client hitting the chatbot/v1/openframe or /api/now/v1/awa endpoints with an email-only identity payload referencing 'Record Management' is high-fidelity for CVE-2025-12420 exploitation. Rationale: Anchored on the two literal strings in code-block 1 ('servicenowexternalagent') and code-block 2 ('Record Management AI Agent') from the Snyk article, plus the AppOmni-disclosed AutoChat / AIA-Agent Invoker pivot path. The combination of a Now-platform host, a Virtual Agent API path, and one of the agent markers is uniquely tied to BodySnatcher exploitation — legitimate chatbot traffic from end users won't carry the 'Record Management' agent name in the request body because that agent is admin-only. Cross-checked against: • https://www.appomni.com/blog/servicenow-virtual-agent-vulnerability • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-12420 • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1190/
ATT&CKT1190 T1078.004 T1136.003
Data sourcesWeb.Web
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Legitimate ServiceNow integration partners (UiPath, Microsoft Copilot Studio, Zendesk) calling the Virtual Agent API with their own scoped OAuth tokens
  • ServiceNow administrators testing the Record Management AI Agent from the platform's own admin UI during patch validation
  • Internal HR/IT self-service portals that embed the Virtual Agent chatbot widget and reference agent names in request bodies
  • AppOmni / Snyk vulnerability scanners specifically probing for CVE-2025-12420
  • ServiceNow's own MID-Server health checks that exercise the AWA API endpoint
Suppression filter
// Allow-list known ServiceNow integration partner egress IPs and the platform's own MID-Server ranges.
| where SourceIP !in ("<approved_integration_partner_cidrs>", "<servicenow_mid_server_cidr>")
| where not (UA has_any ("ServiceNow-MID", "AppOmni-Scanner", "Snyk-Vuln-Scanner"))
Response runbook
Step 1 (Triage): Pull the full HTTP request payload from the WAF / proxy log. Verify it carries an email-only identity claim (no MFA, no SSO assertion) and that the body references 'Record Management' or 'AIA-Agent Invoker'. Cross-correlate the source IP against your inventory of approved ServiceNow integration partners. Step 2 (Contain): Have the ServiceNow admin disable the Virtual Agent third-party API channel, rotate any 'servicenowexternalagent'-style shared credentials, and apply ServiceNow's CVE-2025-12420 hotfix. Block the source IP at the WAF. Step 3 (Hunt): Query the ServiceNow sys_audit table for any sys_user records created or modified by 'AIA-Agent Invoker' in the last 30 days. Enumerate which records the impersonated mailbox can now access in downstream-connected systems (Salesforce, M365) — assume lateral movement until proven otherwise. Force password reset on any admin accounts created post-incident.
// Detect outbound calls from any device to ServiceNow tenants invoking the Virtual Agent / AutoChat API while carrying Record-Management agent indicators (CVE-2025-12420 BodySnatcher).
let SnowHost = "service-now.com";
let ChatAPI = dynamic(["/api/now/v1/awa", "/api/now/cs/", "/api/sn_va_as_service", "virtualagent", "openframe"]);
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteUrl has SnowHost
| where RemoteUrl has_any (ChatAPI)
| where AdditionalFields has_any ("Record Management", "AIA-Agent Invoker", "servicenowexternalagent", "sys_user")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountUpn, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort, AdditionalFields
| order by Timestamp desc
// Detect ServiceNow Virtual Agent / AutoChat API calls that invoke the Record-Management AI Agent (CVE-2025-12420 BodySnatcher).
let SnowHost = "service-now.com";
let ChatPaths = dynamic(["/api/now/v1/awa", "/api/now/cs/", "/api/sn_va_as_service", "virtualagent", "openframe"]);
let AgentMarkers = dynamic(["Record Management", "AIA-Agent Invoker", "servicenowexternalagent", "AutoChat"]);
let WAFHits = CommonSecurityLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where DestinationHostName has SnowHost or RequestURL has SnowHost
    | where RequestURL has_any (ChatPaths)
    | where RequestURL has_any (AgentMarkers)
        or RequestCookies has_any (AgentMarkers)
        or RequestContext has_any (AgentMarkers)
        or Message has_any (AgentMarkers)
        or DeviceCustomString1 has_any (AgentMarkers)
        or DeviceCustomString2 has_any (AgentMarkers)
        or DeviceCustomString3 has_any (AgentMarkers)
        or DeviceCustomString4 has_any (AgentMarkers)
        or DeviceCustomString5 has_any (AgentMarkers)
        or DeviceCustomString6 has_any (AgentMarkers)
    | project TimeGenerated, Source="WAF", SourceIP, DestinationHost=DestinationHostName, Method=RequestMethod, URL=RequestURL, UA=RequestClientApplication, Status=tostring(EventOutcome);
let WebHits = ImWebSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where HttpHostName has SnowHost or Url has SnowHost
    | where Url has_any (ChatPaths) or UrlOriginal has_any (ChatPaths)
    | where Url has_any (AgentMarkers)
        or UrlOriginal has_any (AgentMarkers)
        or HttpRequestHeaderAuthorization has_any (AgentMarkers)
        or HttpRequestHeaderCookie has_any (AgentMarkers)
        or HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent has_any (AgentMarkers)
        or HttpRequestHeaderReferer has_any (AgentMarkers)
    | project TimeGenerated, Source="ASIM-Web", SourceIP=SrcIpAddr, DestinationHost=HttpHostName, Method=HttpMethod, URL=Url, UA=HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent, Status=tostring(HttpStatusCode);
let IISHits = W3CIISLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where csUriStem has_any (ChatPaths)
    | where csUriQuery has_any (AgentMarkers)
        or csUriStem has_any (AgentMarkers)
        or csUserAgent has_any (AgentMarkers)
        or csReferer has_any (AgentMarkers)
    | project TimeGenerated, Source="IIS", SourceIP=cIP, DestinationHost=Computer, Method=csMethod, URL=strcat(csUriStem, "?", csUriQuery), UA=csUserAgent, Status=tostring(scStatus);
union WAFHits, WebHits, IISHits
| order by TimeGenerated desc
(source:cloudtrail OR source:azure.activity_logs) (@http.url:*service-now.com* AND (@http.url:*/api/now/v1/awa* OR @http.url:*/api/now/cs/* OR @http.url:*virtualagent* OR @http.url:*sn_va_as_service*) AND (@http.request.body:(*"Record Management"* OR *"AIA-Agent Invoker"* OR *servicenowexternalagent* OR *AutoChat*) OR @http.url:*servicenowexternalagent*))
#event_simpleName=DnsRequest DomainName=/service-now\.com$/i
| join({#event_simpleName=NetworkConnectIP4}, field=[aid, ContextProcessId])
| join({#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 | regex("(Record Management|AIA-Agent Invoker|servicenowexternalagent|AutoChat)", field=CommandLine, ignoreCase=true)}, field=[aid, ContextProcessId])
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, ContextImageFileName, DomainName, CommandLine], function=count())
WEB

description: |

    pivots over SMB. Pair with `wmic /node:` for the full cluster.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1021/002/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1569/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02

    - attack.t1569.002
    - attack.lateral-movement
    - attack.execution

    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_psexec:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\psexec.exe'
            - '\psexec64.exe'
            - '\psexesvc.exe'
            - '\paexec.exe'
            - '\csexec.exe'
            - '\remcom.exe'
            - '\smbexec.py'
    selection_wmic_remote:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\wmic.exe'
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '/node:'
    condition: selection_psexec or selection_wmic_remote
falsepositives:
    - Sysadmins running approved remote-management tooling — wrap with
      a known-admin-host watchlist
level: high

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes

Phase 7
Actions on Objectives
—
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.

DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

CISA KEV: CVE-2009-0556 — Microsoft Office PowerPoint Code Injection Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2026-01-07 1 use case 0 techniques 2 kill-chain phases detected

Microsoft Office PowerPoint contains a code injection vulnerability that allows remote attackers to execute arbitrary code via a PowerPoint file with an OutlineTextRefAtom containing an invalid index value that triggers memory corruption. Vendor: Microsoft, Product: Office. Federal patch due: 2026-01-28.

ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2009-0556")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2009-0556")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc
